1	\section{Adaptive Methods}\label{sec:adaptive}
2	
3	Since instance-level selection fails (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}), we present approaches that extract value from multi-sample generation beyond single-signal sample ranking.
4	
5	\subsection{Cross-Model Self-Consistency}\label{sec:cross_model}
6	
7	Diversity from stochastic sampling within a single model is limited by architecture-specific degeneracy (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}).
8	Mixing samples from architecturally distinct models---which produce complementary error patterns---can partially bypass this bottleneck.
9	
10	Combining 4 samples each from Qwen3-8B and LLaMA-3.1-8B (both fine-tuned on SciERC), LP-weighted entity construction yields $+0.42$\,pp over pure LLaMA sampling ($p{<}0.001$, 95\% CI $[0.27, 0.58]$; $N{=}8$ total).
11	The mixed ensemble exploits cross-architecture diversity: entities agreed upon by both families carry higher precision than those from either alone.
12	
13	\subsection{Trained Entity-Level Verifier}\label{sec:verifier}
14	
15	We train a LightGBM classifier on entity-level features (agreement count, LP, and consistency signals) to predict entity correctness.
16	Cross-dataset transfer on 7B fine-tuned models is negative (SciERC${\to}$Few-NERD: $-0.96$\,pp; SciERC${\to}$CoNLL: $-1.01$, $-1.64$\,pp vs.\ greedy; Appendix~\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), demonstrating dataset-specific feature mappings.
17	Under 72B zero-shot, transfer is positive ($+1.41$, $+1.51$\,pp) but approximately matches majority vote, suggesting gains derive from threshold calibration rather than learned discrimination.
18	This motivates approaches that generate training signal from the target distribution itself (\S\ref{sec:scd}).
19	
20	\subsection{Self-Consistency Distillation}\label{sec:scd}
21	
22	The verifier's negative transfer motivates an alternative: rather than training a cross-dataset classifier, use each dataset's own multi-sample consensus as supervision.
23	\textbf{Self-consistency distillation} (SCD) f

440	DeBERTa achieves $\rho{=}0.329{\pm}0.087$ (5-fold CV, 3 seeds). Despite moderate quality prediction, selection utility is poor: on SciERC, DeBERTa closes $-26.5{\pm}26.5$\% of the oracle gap (13 of 15 runs negative; vs.\ LP's $+4.2{\pm}10.7$\%); on Few-NERD, DeBERTa recovers only $+3.5$\% (single 80/20 split; vs.\ LP's $+16.3$\%).
441	Cross-dataset transfer fails entirely ($-27$\% to $-72$\% gap closure).
442	Grid-search ensemble optimization over signal weights confirms LP dominance: across configurations, LP receives mean weight $0.78$, while FK and SJ receive zero weight; VC and EM serve only as secondary regularizers.
443	Neither handcrafted signal combinations nor learned text representations close the selection gap; only model-internal log-probability provides meaningful selection signal, consistent with an information asymmetry between the generating model and external observers.
444	
445	\subsection{Entity-Level Verifier Transfer}\label{app:verifier_transfer}
446	
447	A LightGBM entity-level verifier (5 features: agreement count, VC, LP, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.93$ on SciERC (5-fold CV), confirming that entity correctness is learnable from the available signals.
448	Cross-dataset transfer to 7B fine-tuned models on Few-NERD is uniformly negative: $-0.96$, $-1.01$, $-1.64$\,pp across three configurations (mean $-1.2$\,pp), all below greedy.
449	The learned feature--correctness mapping is dataset-specific: entity agreement patterns that predict correctness on scientific text do not transfer to news or fine-grained entity types.
450	On 72B zero-shot, cross-dataset transfer is positive ($+1.41$, $+1.51$\,pp) but does not exceed majority-vote construction, suggesting the verifier recovers diversity gains already available to unsupervised aggregation at this scale.
451	
452	\subsection{Hidden State Probes}\label{app:hidden_probe}
453

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$) but aggregation fails: a degeneracy decomposition ($\theta_{\text{crit}}{=}0.70$; \S\ref{sec:theory}) formalizes when SC becomes futile.
4	Entity-level construction partially closes the gap ($+1.40$\,pp Few-NERD, $+1.02$\,pp SciERC); a trained verifier transfers negatively cross-dataset ($-1.2$\,pp mean on 7B FT)---content-aware methods \cite{cobbe2021verifiers} may bridge this.

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$; \S\ref{sec:scaling}), but aggregation cannot close the gap: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\rho$ 0.043--0.667) constrain it.
4	Temperature disentangles these at $T{=}1.2$ (degeneracy 14.9\%, LP $\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}).
5	
6	\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\label{sec:selection_method}
7	Entity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\% and absent when ${>}31$\% (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}).
8	Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised mixtures fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\ref{app:dals}, \ref{app:supervised_combination}, \ref{app:when_selection_works}).
9

  "old_string": "\\subsection{Trained Entity-Level Verifier}\\label{sec:verifier}\n\nWe train a LightGBM classifier on entity-level features (agreement count, LP, and consistency signals) to predict entity correctness.\nCross-dataset transfer on 7B fine-tuned models is negative (SciERC${\\to}$Few-NERD: $-0.96$\\,pp; SciERC${\\to}$CoNLL: $-1.01$, $-1.64$\\,pp vs.\\ greedy; Appendix~\\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), demonstrating dataset-specific feature mappings.\nUnder 72B zero-shot, transfer is positive ($+1.41$, $+1.51$\\,pp) but approximately matches majority vote, suggesting gains derive from threshold calibration rather than learned discrimination.\nThis motivates approaches that generate training signal from the target distribution itself (\\S\\ref{sec:scd}).",
  "new_string": "\\subsection{Trained Verifiers: High Discrimination, Low Transfer}\\label{sec:verifier}\n\nA LightGBM entity-level classifier (5 features: agreement count, LP, VC, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.923{\\pm}0.002$ under out-of-fold evaluation, confirming that entity correctness is learnable from the available signals.\nYet this discrimination translates poorly to F1: OOF construction yields only $+0.32$\\,pp overall ($0.8248$ vs.\\ $0.8216$ greedy), with per-dataset gains of $+1.28$\\,pp on SciERC, $+0.55$\\,pp on CoNLL, and $+0.02$\\,pp on Few-NERD.\nThe near-zero Few-NERD gain despite AUC${>}0.92$ reveals that frequency-based threshold construction (\\S\\ref{sec:prescriptive}) already captures most of the verifier's signal; SciERC---with low degeneracy and diverse entity-level errors---is the exception where learned discrimination adds value beyond majority vote.\nCross-dataset transfer remains negative (B2-v2: $-0.76$\\,pp; B2-v3: $-1.02$\\,pp on Few-NERD; Appendix~\\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), confirming dataset-specific feature mappings.\nEven a trained discriminator with near-perfect entity classification cannot close the aggregation gap, indicating the bottleneck is structural rather than a matter of signal quality.\nThis motivates approaches that generate training signal from the target distribution itself (\\S\\ref{sec:scd})."

_source line 17781 · 2026-05-20T12:40:04.074111+00:00_

247	Construction and LP selection are complementary, governed by $\rho_{\text{adj}}$: Few-NERD ($\rho_{\text{adj}}{=}0.516$) favors both, while SciERC ($\rho_{\text{adj}}{=}0.167$) favors construction only. A three-way routing protocol ($\rho_{\text{adj}} \geq 0.3$: LP; $<0.3$ with positive construction: construction; else: greedy) is stable across $\tau{\in}[0.2, 0.4]$.
248	
249	\paragraph{Trained entity-level verifier.}
250	Entity verification features achieve AUC${=}0.93$ in-domain (LightGBM on agreement count, LP, and consistency features), improving over greedy by $+3.6$\,pp ($27$\% gap closure).
251	However, the trained verifier shows negative transfer (Few-NERD 7B: $-1.2$\,pp vs.\ greedy; Appendix~\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), demonstrating dataset-specific feature mappings. This motivates distillation approaches using each dataset's own SC consensus as supervision.
252	
253	\paragraph{Selective prediction.}
254	QE signals support selective prediction: at 80\% coverage, filtering low-confidence instances yields $+5$--$8$\,pp F1 (AUPRC 0.70--0.92; Table~\ref{tab:cross_selection}).
255	
256	\phantomsection\label{sec:selection_results}

_source line 17787 · 2026-05-20T12:40:19.495954+00:00_

230	\paragraph{Cross-configuration selection analysis.}
231	Table~\ref{tab:cross_selection} extends the selection analysis to five additional configurations.
232	On LLaMA SciERC, where output diversity remains substantial (18\% identical outputs, 39\% unique), LP achieves the highest selection F1 (0.652) at $N{=}8$ but still falls within bootstrap noise of single-sample (0.646), replicating the gap observed on Qwen SciERC (Table~\ref{tab:selection}).
233	On LLaMA FewNERD ($n{=}32{,}565$, 3-seed mean), LP is the only signal with positive selection ($+0.36$~pp), confirming LP as the most robust selection signal across model families (Qwen FewNERD: $+1.39$~pp, Table~\ref{tab:fewnerd}). All structural signals degrade selection (VC $-2.39$~pp, SJ $-0.52$~pp), consistent with the Qwen pattern. LLaMA FewNERD degeneracy ($23.4{\pm}4.7$\%, 3 seeds) is higher than Qwen ($13.0$\%), illustrating the capability-diversity tension: LLaMA achieves stronger greedy F1 ($0.784$ vs.\ $0.748$, $+3.6$~pp) but at the cost of higher degeneracy ($+10.4$~pp) and reduced oracle headroom ($10.0$ vs.\ $12.4$~pp).
234	On CoNLL, 48--87\% of instances produce identical outputs across all $N$ samples; this reflects a ceiling effect (single-sample F1 ${\approx}$0.91--0.92) rather than signal failure, and selection analysis on these configurations is uninformative because there is little per-sample variance for any signal to exploit.
235

1	\section{Adaptive Methods}\label{sec:adaptive}
2	
3	Since instance-level selection fails (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}), we present approaches that extract value from multi-sample generation beyond single-signal sample ranking.
4	
5	\subsection{Cross-Model Self-Consistency}\label{sec:cross_model}
6	
7	Diversity from stochastic sampling within a single model is limited by architecture-specific degeneracy (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}).
8	Mixing samples from architecturally distinct models---which produce complementary error patterns---can partially bypass this bottleneck.
9	
10	Combining 4 samples each from Qwen3-8B and LLaMA-3.1-8B (both fine-tuned on SciERC), LP-weighted entity construction yields $+0.42$\,pp over pure LLaMA sampling ($p{<}0.001$, 95\% CI $[0.27, 0.58]$; $N{=}8$ total).
11	The mixed ensemble exploits cross-architecture diversity: entities agreed upon by both families carry higher precision than those from either alone.
12	
13	\subsection{Trained Verifiers: High Discrimination, Low Transfer}\label{sec:verifier}
14	
15	A LightGBM entity-level classifier (5 features: agreement count, LP, VC, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.923{\pm}0.002$ under out-of-fold evaluation, confirming that entity correctness is learnable from the available signals.
16	Yet this discrimination translates poorly to F1: OOF construction yields only $+0.32$\,pp overall ($0.8248$ vs.\ $0.8216$ greedy), with per-dataset gains of $+1.28$\,pp on SciERC, $+0.55$\,pp on CoNLL, and $+0.02$\,pp on Few-NERD.
17	The near-zero Few-NERD gain despite AUC${>}0.92$ reveals that frequency-based threshold construction (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}) already captures most of the verifier's signal; SciERC---with low degeneracy and diverse entity-level errors---is the exception where learned discrimination adds value beyond majority vote.
18	Cross-dataset transfer remains negative (B2-v2: $-0.76$\,pp; B2-v3: $-1.02$\,pp on Few-NERD; Appendix~\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), confirming dataset-specific feature mapping
…[truncated 984 chars]

_source line 17829 · 2026-05-20T12:44:09.884962+00:00_

445	\subsection{Entity-Level Verifier Transfer}\label{app:verifier_transfer}
446	
447	A LightGBM entity-level verifier (5 features: agreement count, VC, LP, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.923{\pm}0.002$ under out-of-fold evaluation, confirming that entity correctness is learnable from the available signals.
448	In-domain OOF construction yields $+0.30$\,pp overall, with per-dataset gains of $+1.28$\,pp (SciERC), $+0.55$\,pp (CoNLL), and $+0.02$\,pp (Few-NERD).
449	The near-zero Few-NERD gain despite high AUC shows that frequency-based threshold construction already captures most of the verifier's signal.
450	Cross-dataset transfer to Few-NERD is negative: $-0.76$\,pp (B2-v2) and $-1.02$\,pp (B2-v3), both below greedy.
451	The learned feature--correctness mapping is dataset-specific: entity agreement patterns that predict correctness on scientific text do not transfer to fine-grained entity types.
452	
453	\subsection{Hidden State Probes}\label{app:hidden_probe}
454	
455	The preceding negative results suggest the bottleneck is model-internal rather than a feature engineering problem.
456	We test this directly by training probes on the generating model's hidden representations.

_source line 17839 · 2026-05-20T13:24:17.761986+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch — R25 审稿回应, Priority 1 framing fixes]

R25 审稿结果 3.5/3.5 (Borderline Conference)。两位 Reviewer 的核心问题全在 framing/positioning，不在科学内容。以下修改是 3.5→4.0 的关键路径。

**EMNLP 2026 Main Conference, DDL 2026-05-25. 引用需要验证。**

**修改 1 — 收紧 structured prediction framing [R25-R2-N1, critical]**
Abstract, Introduction, 以及论文各处将 "structured prediction" / "structured IE" 的声称收紧为 NER-specific。
- Abstract: "set-valued structured prediction task" → "NER and similar set-valued extraction tasks"
- Introduction §1: 第5行 "any set-valued structured prediction task" → "NER and related set-valued extraction tasks; we hypothesize similar patterns in other structured IE tasks but verify only NER here"
- 注意保留 "set-valued" 的概念贡献性表述，但限制 empirical scope claim
- Limitations 中已有 "four NER datasets" limitation，确认不需改动

**修改 2 — 分离 definitional gap vs empirical gap [R25-R1-N1, critical]**
§4 (diagnostic section) 开头增加一段明确区分：
- **Definitional gap**: 4/5 signals (SJ, VC, FK, EM) are instance-level aggregates → cannot rank candidates from the same input by construction. This is not a surprise.
- **Empirical gap**: (a) Instance-level filtering also fails (Table showing LP is the only signal achieving significant filtering gains); (b) LP, the only per-sample signal, suffers compression (63% instances have within-instance LP range < 0.041 nat). These ARE empirical findings.
- Introduction 中 Contribution 1 的叙事也需同步调整：强调 empirical contribution 在于 LP compression 和 instance-level filtering failure，而非 definitional observation

**修改 3 — Entity construction 更诚实的呈现 [R25-R1-W1 + R2-N2]**
当前 §5.3 和 Contribution 3 需更显著地呈现 generalizability 限制：
- 在 entity construction 结果段（§5.3/prescriptive 部分），将 SciERC p_adj=0.20 和 CoNLL -0.29pp 与 Few-NERD +1.40pp 并列呈现
- 添加一句明确讨论 applicability conditions: "Entity construction yields significant gains only when degeneracy is low AND oracle headroom is sufficient (Few-NERD: D=13%, headroom=12.4pp); on datasets where either condition fails, construction is ineffective."
- Contribution 3 确认保持 D192 的 "diagnostic-motivated mitigation" 定位，不作为独立方法贡献

**修改 4 — 三种故障模式 → diagnostic cascade [R25-R2-W2]**
§4 中 "three structural failure modes" 改为 diagnostic cascade：
- "We identify a diagnostic cascade: (1) instance degeneracy sets the ceiling on possible improvement; (2) within non-degenerate instances, oracle headroom distribution determines the opportunity; (3) LP compression limits exploitation of that opportunity."
- 不再暗示三者可独立诊断/解决

**修改 5 — LP compression 链接 calibration 文献 [R25-R2-W1]**
Discussion 中增加一段（~3-4 句），将 LP compression 与 LLM calibration 文献对接：
- Reference Kadavath et al. 2022 (已引用), Xiong et al. 2024 uncertainty quantification survey
- 解释 teacher-forcing exposure bias, constrained decoding 对 LP dynamic range 的压缩
- 指向 potential remedies (per-token LP variance, calibration-aware scoring) as future work

**修改 6 — n=23 措辞软化 + power analysis [R25-R1-N2]**
§6.3 或 §4 中 cross-dataset ρ=0.052 结论：
- "degeneracy does not predict LP gain" → "we did not find evidence that degeneracy predicts LP gain (ρ=0.052, n=23; post-hoc power analysis: 80% power requires n≈85 for ρ=0.3)"
- Appendix 补充 power analysis table (n=23 的 power at various ρ values)

_source line 17841 · 2026-05-20T13:24:38.477696+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Test-time compute scaling---repeated sampling with selection or aggregation---yields large gains on discrete-answer reasoning and code generation, but named entity recognition (NER) produces \textit{sets} of entities with partial overlap, where standard aggregation mechanisms break down.
3	We evaluate five confidence signals for quality estimation (QE) and sample selection on four NER benchmarks across fine-tuned 4B\textendash 8B and zero-shot 72B models.
4	All signals achieve reliable QE ($\rho$ up to 0.45 on CoNLL-2003, area under the ROC curve [AUROC] up to 0.82), enabling selective prediction ($+5$--$8$\,pp F1 at 80\% coverage).
5	For instance-level selection, log-probability (LP) succeeds only where LP-quality alignment is strong, and gains are absent elsewhere despite large oracle headroom (the \textbf{correlation-selection gap}).
6	We diagnose three structural failure modes---instance degeneracy, limited oracle headroom, and LP compression---that persist at 72B zero-shot scale, providing the first systematic characterization of when test-time scaling succeeds or fails for structured prediction.
7	The diagnostic motivates entity-level \textit{construction}, adapting entity voting to stochastic sampling: LP-weighted aggregation improves F1 by $+1.40$\,pp on Few-NERD and $+1.02$\,pp on SciERC under fine-tuning (multi-seed mean, $p{<}0.005$). Construction and LP selection are complementary, governed by LP-quality alignment; a degeneracy decomposition confirms that SC gain vanishes when degeneracy exceeds 70\%.
8	\end{abstract}
9

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time compute scaling has emerged as a complement to model scaling \cite{snell2025scaling}: repeated sampling with majority voting \citep{wang2023selfconsistency}, best-of-$N$ with learned verifiers \cite{cobbe2021verifiers, brown2024monkeys}, and extended chain-of-thought \cite{muennighoff2025s1} yield large gains on reasoning and code generation, but the evidence concentrates on tasks with discrete answers or execution-based verification.
4	Named entity recognition (NER) poses a structurally different challenge: the output is a \textit{set} of entities, two samples may extract overlapping but non-identical sets, and there is no obvious majority to vote on.
5	This challenge generalizes to any \textit{set-valued} structured prediction task where outputs admit partial but not exact overlap.
6	
7	Prior work on confidence estimation for LLM-based IE has conflated \textit{quality estimation} (which inputs does the model handle reliably?) with \textit{sample selection} (which of $N$ outputs is best?).
8	Existing self-consistency methods \cite{wang2023selfconsistency, chen2024usc, taubenfeld2025cisc, kang2025selfcertainty} assume outputs can be grouped by equivalence, an assumption that breaks for entity sets with partial overlap.
9	Unlike \citet{yao2026consistency}, who demonstrate that self-consistency correlates with task accuracy, we provide the first systematic empirical characterization of \textit{when and why} test-time scaling via LP-based selection succeeds or fails for structured prediction, diagnosing degeneracy and compression as the two blocking mechanisms.
10	
11	A key distinction structures our analysis: four of five signals (Soft Jaccard, voting confidence, Fleiss' $\kappa$, exact match) are instance-level aggregates that assign identical scores to all $N$ candidates for a given input; only log-probability (LP) is natively per-sample.
12	All signals achieve reliable quality estimation ($\rho$ up to 
…[truncated 3540 chars]

1	\section{Why Single-Signal Selection Fails}\label{sec:selection_gap}
2	
3	Four of five signals assign identical scores to all candidates from the same input by construction (\S\ref{sec:formulation}); they estimate quality across instances but cannot rank candidates within an instance.
4	LP selection effectiveness depends on LP-quality alignment rather than degeneracy alone.
5	Cross-instance $\rho$ (Table~\ref{tab:quality_estimation}) is attenuated by varying instance difficulty; within-instance $\rho$ directly measures whether LP discriminates candidates for a fixed input, making it the relevant predictor of selection success.

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$; \S\ref{sec:scaling}), but aggregation cannot close the gap: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\rho$ 0.043--0.667) constrain it.
4	Temperature disentangles these at $T{=}1.2$ (degeneracy 14.9\%, LP $\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}).
5	
6	\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\label{sec:selection_method}
7	Entity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\% and absent when ${>}31$\% (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}).
8	Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised mixtures fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\ref{app:dals}, \ref{app:supervised_combination}, \ref{app:when_selection_works}).
9

1	\section{Theory \& Scaling Law}\label{sec:theory_scaling}
2	
3	\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\label{sec:mbr_theory}
4	
5	Entity-level construction (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\arg\max_{y \in \{y_1,\ldots,y_N\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\cup{=}\bigcup_i y_i$.
6	
7	\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\label{prop:construction_mbr}
8	Under entity-wise independence with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is
9	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mbr_construction}
10	\hat{y}_{\textup{MBR}} \!=\! \bigl\{e \!{\in}\! E_\cup \!:\! \hat{p}(e) \!>\! \tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha{+}1}\bigr\},\; \hat{p}(e) \!=\! \tfrac{1}{N} \textstyle\sum\nolimits_{i} \mathbf{1}[e \!{\in}\! y_i]
11	\end{equation}
12	The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields $\hat{p}(e) > \tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_proof}).
13	\end{proposition}
14	
15	\noindent\textbf{Remark.}
16	Set-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\cup| \leq 30$).
17	
18	Signal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\hat{p}_w(e) = \sum_{i} w_i \, \mathbf{1}[e {\in} y_i] / \sum_{i} w_i$.
19	Weighted MBR improves when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\rho{\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \S\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}).
20	
21	\subsection{Scaling Behavior}\label{sec:scaling}
22

…[truncated 4189 chars]

1	Our evaluation covers four NER datasets; an exploratory RE extension on SciERC is reported in Appendix~\ref{app:re_extension} but generalization to other RE benchmarks remains unvalidated.
2	All experiments use English-language datasets; cross-lingual generalization remains untested.
3	Entity-level construction assumes the model produces useful partial outputs across samples; when model capability is insufficient (WNUT-17, 51\% F1${=}0$), this precondition fails and construction cannot improve over greedy.
4	We cover 4B\textendash 8B fine-tuned and 72B zero-shot; the gap persists at 72B but behavior with closed-source APIs or 72B fine-tuning is untested.
5	The 72B zero-shot comparison simultaneously varies model family (Qwen3$\to$Qwen2.5), training regime (fine-tuned$\to$zero-shot), and quantization (FP16$\to$AWQ 4-bit); these factors cannot be fully disentangled.
6	Our supervised approaches (DeBERTa verifier, hidden state probes, MLP/RF on signal combinations) all fail to close the oracle gap; whether content-aware verifiers with access to task-specific structure could succeed remains open.
7	
8	We evaluate at $N{=}8$ (primary) and $N{=}16$; behavior at larger sample sizes is unknown (Appendix~\ref{app:n_scaling}).
9	The free-form ablation covers SciERC NER and CoNLL NER with one model; RE free-form results are in Appendix~\ref{app:re_extension}.
10	Qwen3-8B's SciERC checkpoint used empty \texttt{&lt;think>&lt;/think>} blocks; a three-part analysis rules out this confound (Appendix~\ref{app:thinking_token}).
11	LP compression worsens on easy datasets at lower temperature (CoNLL tied fractions reach 95.8\% at $T{=}0.7$); whether reduced temperature translates to selection gains remains untested.
12	We tested verbalized confidence \cite{lin2022teaching, tian2023calibration} as a second per-sample signal; it exhibits severe overconfidence (mean 8.58/10 on SciERC, 9.24/10 on CoNLL) and yields the weakest QE and selection among all signals (Appendix~\ref{app:verbalized_conf
…[truncated 2344 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	\subsection{Experimental Setup}\label{sec:setup}
4	
5	\paragraph{Datasets.}
6	We evaluate on four English NER benchmarks spanning a difficulty gradient.
7	\textbf{SciERC} \cite{luan2018scierc} (551 test instances, 6 entity types) is the primary medium-difficulty test bed (${\approx}$65\% F1).
8	\textbf{Few-NERD} \cite{ding2021fewnerd} (INTRA coarse; 37{,}648 instances, 8 entity types, ${\approx}$75\% F1) provides a low-degeneracy contrast where LP selection succeeds (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}).
9	\textbf{CoNLL-2003} \cite{tjongkimsang2003conll} (3{,}453 test sentences, 4 entity types, ${\approx}$91\% F1) serves as a ceiling-effect control.
10	\textbf{WNUT-17} \cite{derczynski2017wnut} (1{,}287 social media instances, 51\% zero F1) probes the signal-collapse boundary.
11	
12	\paragraph{Models and sampling.}
13	\textbf{Qwen3-8B} and \textbf{LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct} are LoRA fine-tuned using LLaMA Factory \cite{zheng2024llamafactory} (Appendix~\ref{app:training}; Few-NERD uses 3 epochs vs.\ 5 for others; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}).
14	$N{=}8$ samples at $T{=}1.0$ (primary) with vLLM constrained decoding \cite{willard2023guided}; $N{=}16$ for scaling (Appendix~\ref{app:n_scaling}).
15

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$; \S\ref{sec:scaling}), but aggregation cannot close the gap: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\rho$ 0.043--0.667) constrain it.
4	Temperature disentangles these at $T{=}1.2$ (degeneracy 14.9\%, LP $\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}).
5	
6	\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\label{sec:selection_method}
7	Entity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\% and absent when ${>}31$\% (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}).
8	Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised mixtures fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\ref{app:dals}, \ref{app:supervised_combination}, \ref{app:when_selection_works}).
9

  "new_string": "We identify a diagnostic cascade---instance degeneracy sets the ceiling on improvement, oracle headroom determines the opportunity, and LP compression limits exploitation---that persists under our 72B zero-shot configuration, providing the first systematic characterization of when test-time scaling succeeds or fails for NER and similar set-valued extraction tasks."
}
```

  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex",
  "old_string": "    \\item \\textbf{Failure modes of test-time scaling for structured prediction.}\n    LP-quality alignment is the primary predictor of selection success ($+1.39$~pp on Few-NERD, $p{<}0.001$); instance degeneracy causally modulates LP within fixed settings but does not predict cross-dataset direction ($\\rho{=}0.052$, 23 configs).\n    The resulting three-step diagnostic provides a reusable protocol for structured test-time scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).",
  "new_string": "    \\item \\textbf{Failure modes of test-time scaling for NER.}\n    That 4/5 signals are instance-level is definitional; the empirical contribution is that LP---the only per-sample signal---suffers compression (60\\% of instances have within-instance LP range ${<}0.041$ nats) and that LP-quality alignment is the primary predictor of selection success ($+1.39$~pp on Few-NERD, $p{<}0.001$).\n    The resulting diagnostic cascade provides a reusable protocol for set-valued test-time scaling (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion})."
}
```

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/single_signal.tex",
  "old_string": "Four of five signals assign identical scores to all candidates from the same input by construction (\\S\\ref{sec:formulation}); they estimate quality across instances but cannot rank candidates within an instance.\nLP selection effectiveness depends on LP-quality alignment rather than degeneracy alone.\nCross-instance $\\rho$ (Table~\\ref{tab:quality_estimation}) is attenuated by varying instance difficulty; within-instance $\\rho$ directly measures whether LP discriminates candidates for a fixed input, making it the relevant predictor of selection success.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Definitional vs.\\ empirical.}\nThat four of five signals assign identical scores to all candidates from the same input is definitional (\\S\\ref{sec:formulation}): instance-level aggregates estimate quality across inputs but cannot rank candidates within an instance.\nThe empirical findings are twofold: (a)~LP---the only per-sample signal---suffers compression under constrained decoding (median within-instance range 0.041 nats; 60\\% of SciERC instances below this threshold; Appendix~\\ref{app:logprob_ties}), and (b)~even where LP varies, selection gains depend on LP-quality alignment rather than degeneracy alone.\nCross-instance $\\rho$ (Table~\\ref{tab:quality_estimation}) is attenuated by varying instance difficulty; within-instance $\\rho$ directly measures whether LP discriminates candidates for a fixed input, making it the relevant predictor of selection success."
}
```

</details>

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/single_signal.tex",
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Three governing factors.}\n(1)~Instance degeneracy (SciERC 17.8\\%, CoNLL 61.7\\%); (2)~oracle headroom (CoNLL ${\\approx}4$\\,pp vs.\\ SciERC 13.0\\,pp); (3)~LP-quality alignment (Few-NERD within-instance $\\rho{\\approx}0.5$--$0.7$ vs.\\ SciERC $\\rho{\\approx}0.26$).\nAcross 23 configurations, degeneracy does not predict LP gain ($\\rho{=}0.052$, $p{=}0.81$; Figure~\\ref{fig:degeneracy_scatter}), though it causally modulates LP within fixed settings ($\\rho{=}{-}0.881$; Appendix~\\ref{app:per_type_lp}).\nThe factors do not combine into a predictive model ($R^2{<}0$) but each is supported by controlled ablations (\\S\\ref{sec:fewnerd}, \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Diagnostic cascade.}\nWe identify a cascade rather than independent factors:\n(1)~instance degeneracy sets the ceiling on possible improvement (SciERC 17.8\\%, CoNLL 61.7\\%);\n(2)~within non-degenerate instances, oracle headroom determines the opportunity (CoNLL ${\\approx}4$\\,pp vs.\\ SciERC 13.0\\,pp);\n(3)~LP compression limits exploitation of that opportunity (Few-NERD within-instance $\\rho{\\approx}0.5$--$0.7$ vs.\\ SciERC $\\rho{\\approx}0.26$).\nWe did not find evidence that degeneracy alone predicts LP gain across datasets ($\\rho{=}0.052$, $n{=}23$, $p{=}0.81$; post-hoc power: 80\\% power requires $n{\\approx}85$ for $\\rho{=}0.3$; Figure~\\ref{fig:degeneracy_scatter}), though degeneracy causally modulates LP within fixed settings ($\\rho{=}{-}0.881$; Appendix~\\ref{app:per_type_lp}).\nThe cascade stages interact rather than combining into a predictive model ($R^2{<}0$); each is supported by controlled ablations (\\S\\ref{sec:fewnerd}, \\S\\ref{sec:discussion})."
}
```

</details>

    "new_string": "\\paragraph{Degeneracy alone does not predict LP direction.}\nWe did not find evidence that degeneracy predicts LP gain across 23 configurations ($\\rho{=}0.052$, $p{=}0.81$; post-hoc: 80\\% power requires $n{\\approx}85$ for $\\rho{=}0.3$; Figure~\\ref{fig:degeneracy_scatter}), though it causally modulates LP within fixed settings ($\\rho{=}{-}0.881$; Appendix~\\ref{app:per_type_lp}). Cross-dataset prediction requires LP signal quality (Appendix~\\ref{app:conditional_decomp})."
  }
}

  "new_string": "Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised mixtures fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\\ref{app:dals}, \\ref{app:supervised_combination}, \\ref{app:when_selection_works}).\n\n\\paragraph{LP compression and calibration.}\nLP compression connects to broader calibration challenges in fine-tuned LLMs \\cite{kadavath2022language, xiong2024verbalized}: teacher-forcing training and constrained decoding compress the LP dynamic range, leaving within-instance variation insufficient for sample discrimination (median LP range 0.041 nats on SciERC; Appendix~\\ref{app:logprob_ties}).\n\\citet{yun2025price} observe similar diversity collapse under structured output formats.\nPotential remedies---per-token LP variance, calibration-aware scoring, or temperature-adjusted LP---remain untested for entity-level ranking."
}
```

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$) but aggregation fails: a degeneracy decomposition ($\theta_{\text{crit}}{=}0.70$; \S\ref{sec:theory}) formalizes when SC becomes futile.
4	Entity-level construction partially closes the gap ($+1.40$\,pp Few-NERD, $+1.02$\,pp SciERC); a trained verifier achieves AUC${=}0.92$ but yields only $+0.30$\,pp via OOF construction, with negative cross-dataset transfer---content-aware methods \cite{cobbe2021verifiers} may bridge this.
5

_source line 17909 · 2026-05-20T13:29:02.898863+00:00_

1585	\subsection{Conditional Decomposition of LP Selection}\label{app:conditional_decomp}
1586	
1587	To test whether LP-quality alignment drives selection gains within datasets, we restrict LP selection to the favorable subset: non-degenerate instances whose within-instance LP range exceeds the dataset-specific median (SciERC: 0.040 nats, CoNLL: 0.062, Few-NERD: 0.067).
1588	Table~\ref{tab:conditional_decomp} reports results across three datasets.
1589	
1590	\begin{table}[h]
1591	\centering
1592	\small
1593	\caption{LP selection on the favorable subset (non-degenerate instances with LP range above the dataset-specific median) per dataset (Qwen3-8B, $N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$). CI: bootstrap 95\% confidence interval.}
1594	\label{tab:conditional_decomp}
1595	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{
1596	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
1597	\toprule
1598	Dataset & LP $\Delta$ (pp) & 95\% CI & Interpretation \\
1599	\midrule
1600	SciERC & $+$2.47 & [$+$0.30, $+$4.70] & significant positive \\
1601	Few-NERD & $+$0.63 & [$-$0.02, $+$1.30] & marginal positive \\
1602	CoNLL & $-$1.78 & [$-$2.83, $-$0.76] & significant negative \\
1603	\bottomrule
1604	\end{tabular}
1605	}
1606	\end{table}
1607	
1608	On SciERC, restricting to the favorable subset recovers a significant LP gain, consistent with LP-quality alignment driving selection within the non-degenerate pool.
1609	On Few-NERD, the trend is directionally consistent but the confidence interval crosses zero; the full-dataset result ($+1.39$~pp, $p{<}0.001$) reflects the larger sample size rather than a stronger per-instance effect.
1610	CoNLL remains negative even on the favorable subset, consistent with the ceiling-regime interpretation: at $>$90\% greedy F1, residual errors are idiosyncratic and LP cannot rank corrections.
1611	
1612	\subsection{Entity-Token LP Decomposition}\label{app:entity_token_lp}
1613	
1614	Full-sequence LP averages over all output tokens, diluting entity-relevant signal with formatting and schema tokens.

[Director 指令] [URGENT Supplementary Patch — R25 items missed from first dispatch]

正式审稿报告已到。以下是之前 dispatch 遗漏的 CRITICAL 修改，**与之前的 7 项并行处理，优先级等同**：

**修改 8 — 删除 §5.3 SCD [R25-W3, CONSENSUS CRITICAL]**
§5.3 "Self-Consistency Distillation" 标注 "evaluation is ongoing" 且无任何实验结果。ARR 提交中出现 "ongoing" 工作会被 AC 判为 premature。**立即删除整个 §5.3 subsection**。

**修改 9 — §5.1 Cross-Model SC 降级 [R25-W3, CONSENSUS CRITICAL]**
§5.1 标注 "preliminary" 且仅 SciERC 一个数据集有初步结果 (+0.42pp)。两个选择：
- (a) **推荐**: 保留 §5.1 但删除 "preliminary" 标注，将实验限定为 "exploratory" 或 "initial evidence"（当前数据足以说明方向但不足以做 claim）
- (b) 整段移至 Discussion/Future Work

选 (a)。修改 §5.1 首句：删除 "In preliminary experiments"，改为 "In an exploratory extension combining..."，并在 Limitations 中加一句 "cross-model results are limited to one dataset and one model pair."

**修改 10 — Figure 10 占位符 [VISUAL CRITICAL]**
Appendix 中 Figure 10 (page 31) 是文字占位符："[ Figure placeholder: Oracle F1 (N) scaling curves ]..."。**必须替换为实际图表或删除该 figure reference**。如果 oracle scaling 数据存在，生成图表；如果不存在，删除 Figure 10 及其引用。这会被审稿人视为论文未完成。

**修改 11 — Yao et al. (2026) 差异化 [R25-W14, CONSENSUS WARNING]**
Related Work §2 中增加与 Yao et al. (2026) 的 head-to-head 段落（2-3 句）。关键差异：Yao et al. demonstrate SC correlates with task accuracy; we provide the first diagnostic characterization of WHEN and WHY LP-based selection succeeds or fails, identifying degeneracy and compression as the two blocking mechanisms.（Introduction 第 9 行已有类似表述，RW 需呼应）

**修改 12 — Self-Certainty/P(True) 讨论 [R25-W11, CONSENSUS WARNING]**
Limitations 中已有一句。在 Related Work 或 Discussion 中增加 2-3 句实质性讨论：
- Self-Certainty (Kang et al., 2025) produces per-sample scores via token-level self-evaluation
- P(True) (Kadavath et al., 2022) asks the model to assess its own correctness
- Both are per-sample signals that could in principle bypass the compression bottleneck; whether they suffer similar degradation under fine-tuning is an open question
- 引用 arXiv:2502.18581 (Self-Certainty for Best-of-N) 如果验证存在

完成以上修改后，与之前 7 项一起 compile 确认 8 页。

1187	\paragraph{Oracle ceiling scaling law.}
1188	Oracle F1 follows $F_1(N) = a - b \cdot N^{-c}$, with $R^2 > 0.999$ across all model--dataset fits (Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_scaling}).
1189	The exponent $c$ varies with dataset complexity: FewNERD ($c{\approx}0.61$) scales comparably to reasoning-task estimates ($c{\approx}0.5$--$0.7$; \citealp{brown2024monkeys}), while SciERC converges more slowly ($c{\approx}0.35$), consistent with complex IE producing less marginal diversity per additional sample.
1190	CoNLL's narrow headroom ($b{=}0.04$--$0.10$) makes $c$ unreliable (inter-model variance $2.5{\times}$); we report only $a$ and $b$ for CoNLL.
1191	A single stochastic sample ($N{=}1$, $T{=}1.0$) underperforms greedy by 1--3\,pp because sampling noise outweighs diversity; $N{\geq}4$ is needed to match greedy.

_source line 17959 · 2026-05-20T13:35:07.257468+00:00_

1	\section{Adaptive Methods}\label{sec:adaptive}
2	
3	Since instance-level selection fails (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}), we present approaches that extract value from multi-sample generation beyond single-signal sample ranking.
4	
5	\subsection{Cross-Model Self-Consistency}\label{sec:cross_model}
6	
7	Diversity from stochastic sampling within a single model is limited by architecture-specific degeneracy (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}).
8	Mixing samples from architecturally distinct models---which produce complementary error patterns---can partially bypass this bottleneck.
9	
10	In preliminary experiments combining 4 samples each from Qwen3-8B and LLaMA-3.1-8B (both fine-tuned on SciERC), LP-weighted entity construction yields $+0.42$\,pp over pure LLaMA sampling ($p{<}0.001$, 95\% CI $[0.27, 0.58]$; $N{=}8$ total), suggesting cross-architecture diversity can partially compensate for within-model degeneracy.
11	
12	\subsection{Trained Verifiers: High Discrimination, Low Transfer}\label{sec:verifier}
13	
14	A LightGBM entity-level classifier (5 features: agreement count, LP, VC, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.923{\pm}0.002$ under out-of-fold evaluation, confirming that entity correctness is learnable from the available signals.
15	Yet this discrimination translates poorly to F1: OOF construction yields only $+0.30$\,pp overall, with per-dataset gains of $+1.28$\,pp on SciERC, $+0.55$\,pp on CoNLL, and $+0.02$\,pp on Few-NERD.
16	The near-zero Few-NERD gain despite AUC${>}0.92$ reveals that frequency-based threshold construction (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}) already captures most of the verifier's signal; SciERC---with low degeneracy and diverse entity-level errors---is the exception where learned discrimination adds value beyond majority vote.
17	Cross-dataset transfer remains negative (B2-v2: $-0.76$\,pp; B2-v3: $-1.02$\,pp on Few-NERD; Appendix~\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), confirming dataset-specific feature mappings.
18	Even a trained discriminator with near-perfect entity cla
…[truncated 921 chars]

_source line 17967 · 2026-05-20T13:35:33.835166+00:00_

1	Our evaluation covers four NER datasets; an exploratory RE extension on SciERC is reported in Appendix~\ref{app:re_extension} but generalization to other RE benchmarks remains unvalidated.
2	All experiments use English-language datasets; cross-lingual generalization remains untested.
3	Entity-level construction assumes the model produces useful partial outputs across samples; when model capability is insufficient (WNUT-17, 51\% F1${=}0$), this precondition fails and construction cannot improve over greedy.
4	We cover 4B\textendash 8B fine-tuned and 72B zero-shot; the gap persists at 72B but behavior with closed-source APIs or 72B fine-tuning is untested.
5	The 72B zero-shot comparison simultaneously varies model family (Qwen3$\to$Qwen2.5), training regime (fine-tuned$\to$zero-shot), and quantization (FP16$\to$AWQ 4-bit); these factors cannot be fully disentangled.

_source line 17971 · 2026-05-20T13:35:47.304949+00:00_

1	\section{Background \& Related Work}\label{sec:related}
2	
3	\paragraph{Test-time compute scaling.}
4	Test-time compute scaling encompasses three families: (a) extended chain-of-thought reasoning \cite{muennighoff2025s1}, (b) repeated sampling with majority voting (self-consistency; \citealp{wang2023selfconsistency}) and its extensions to free-form text \cite{chen2024usc, thirukovalluru2024atomic, manakul2023selfcheckgpt}, and (c) best-of-$N$ selection via compute-optimal allocation \cite{snell2025scaling, brown2024monkeys}.
5	Reward-free selectors \cite{taubenfeld2025cisc, kang2025selfcertainty, wang2025rankedvoting, oh2026latentsc} and adaptive gating variants \cite{aggarwal2023adaptive, wang2025make, feng2025optimal, kim2026reasc} reduce inference cost; \citet{chen2025twofailures} diagnose failure modes.
6	All target discrete-answer reasoning; structured prediction requires different aggregation mechanisms because degeneracy operates at the entity-set level.
7	
8	\paragraph{Consistency for structured IE.}
9	\citet{yao2026consistency} show consistency correlates with accuracy for LLM-based NER/RE; we show correlation alone does not ensure selection gains, diagnosing degeneracy and compression as blocking mechanisms.
10	CoCoA \cite{vashurin2025cocoa} fuses confidence with consistency; our supervised baseline confirms combination is not the bottleneck (\S\ref{sec:robustness_main}).
11	Earlier work on confidence for structured prediction \cite{culotta2004confidence, mejer2010confidence, fomicheva2020unsupervised} establishes the paradigm we extend to set-valued outputs.
12	Ensemble approaches aggregate predictions from multiple models or prompts \cite{florian2003named, xiao2025el4ner}; \citet{xie2023zeroshot} propose two-stage entity voting for zero-shot NER, the closest prior work to our entity-level construction.
13	LLM-Blender \cite{jiang2023llmblender} fuses outputs from multiple LLMs via pairwise ranking, whereas our single-model stochastic sampling derives di
…[truncated 2774 chars]

_source line 17975 · 2026-05-20T13:36:07.175784+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	NER exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$; \S\ref{sec:scaling}), but aggregation cannot close the gap: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\rho$ 0.043--0.667) constrain it.
4	Temperature disentangles these at $T{=}1.2$ (degeneracy 14.9\%, LP $\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}).
5	
6	\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\label{sec:selection_method}
7	Entity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\% and absent when ${>}31$\% (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}).
8	Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised mixtures fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\ref{app:dals}, \ref{app:supervised_combination}, \ref{app:when_selection_works}).
9	
10	\paragraph{LP compression and calibration.}
11	LP compression connects to broader calibration challenges in fine-tuned LLMs \cite{kadavath2022language, xiong2024verbalized}: teacher-forcing training and constrained decoding compress the LP dynamic range, leaving within-instance variation insufficient for sample discrimination (median LP range 0.041 nats on SciERC; Appendix~\ref{app:logprob_ties}).
12	\citet{yun2025price} observe similar diversity collapse under structured output formats.
13	Potential remedies---per-token LP variance, calibration-aware scoring, or temperature-adjusted LP---remain untested for entity-level ranking.
14

[Director 指令] [Patch — URGENT 补充: Phase Transition Theory §6 重构 + Title 修改]

在你正在处理的 12 项 R25 修改之外，用户新增了一个 high-priority 指令。请在完成当前修改后立刻执行。

## 1. Title 修改

从:
"Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors: Addressing the Correlation-Selection Gap in Self-Consistency for Named Entity Recognition"

改为:
"Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors: The Correlation-Selection Gap in Self-Consistency for Named Entity Recognition"

理由: construction 已降级为 diagnostic-motivated mitigation，"Addressing" 暗示 resolution 过强。

## 2. Phase Transition Theory — §6 重构

将现有 §6 的三个独立 subsection (MBR, Scaling, Degeneracy Decomposition) 重构为 unified "Phase Transitions in Threshold-Based Aggregation" 叙事。

**§6.1 MBR Foundation** (保留现有 Proposition 1 不变)
- Balanced linear utility u(ŷ, y') = |ŷ ∩ y'| - α|ŷ \ y'|
- Bayes-optimal: ŷ = {e : p̂(e) > α/(α+1)}
- α=1 → threshold 1/2 (majority vote)

**§6.2 Threshold Sensitivity and Scaling** (合并现有 scaling + inverse scaling)
- 定义 operating threshold θ = k/N (k = minimum agreement count)
- 当 θ 远低于 MBR-optimal threshold θ_c = 1/2 时，precision 随 N 下降 → inverse scaling
- 经验验证: θ=2/N 触发 40/58 significant inverse scaling cases; θ=1/2 (majority vote) 无 inverse scaling
- Oracle F1 仍按 F(N) = a - b·N^{-c} scaling (R²>0.999)
- **注意: 不要称之为 "Phase Transition Theorem"**——这是 empirical observation + MBR 推论的联系，不是 formal theorem。可写成 "Empirical observation" 或 "Corollary"

**§6.3 Degeneracy Decomposition** (保留现有 bound，重新定位)
- E[ΔF1] ≤ (1-D)·G_nd·α
- θ_crit = 0.70
- **明确定位为 diagnostic**（识别 α 为 bottleneck）**而非 predictive**（实际恢复率 3-12%）
- 加一句 tightening 方向讨论

**叙事线索**: §6.1 建立 MBR 决策论基础 → §6.2 展示阈值选择如何影响 N-scaling 行为（连接到实验观测）→ §6.3 给出即使在最优阈值下 gain 仍被限制的 bound（连接到 degeneracy bottleneck）。三者形成 "foundation → dynamics → ceiling" 的递进结构。

**空间约束**: 主文 ≤ 0.8 页。Scaling table (Table in current §6.2) 可能需要移到 appendix 以腾空间。Proof 和详细推导已在 appendix。

## 3. §4 Two-Tier Gap 结构确认

确认用户对 §4 重构的具体要求（你的 12 项修改中已有 definitional vs empirical gap 分离，这里是更精确的规格）:

§4.1 Definitional Gap: 
- SJ, VC, FK, EM 是 instance-level signals，§3.1 已定义它们对 N 个候选打相同分数
- Selection 失效是数学推论，不是经验发现
- 但 instance-level filtering 失效（LP +1.30pp on FewNERD, others insig）IS 经验发现

§4.2 Empirical Gap (LP):
- LP 确实 per-sample 变化
- 但 63% instances within-instance LP range < 0.041 nat → compression
- 这是真正的 empirical "compression" 现象

Abstract/Intro 中删除对 instance-level signals 的 "surprising"/"diagnose" 框定。

## 优先级

当前 12 项修改 > Title > §4 two-tier 精修 > §6 Phase Transition > 其他

1	\section{Why Single-Signal Selection Fails}\label{sec:selection_gap}
2	
3	\paragraph{Definitional vs.\ empirical.}
4	That four of five signals assign identical scores to all candidates from the same input is definitional (\S\ref{sec:formulation}): instance-level aggregates estimate quality across inputs but cannot rank candidates within an instance.
5	The empirical findings are twofold: (a)~LP---the only per-sample signal---suffers compression under constrained decoding (median within-instance range 0.041 nats; 60\% of SciERC instances below this threshold; Appendix~\ref{app:logprob_ties}), and (b)~even where LP varies, selection gains depend on LP-quality alignment rather than degeneracy alone.
6	Cross-instance $\rho$ (Table~\ref{tab:quality_estimation}) is attenuated by varying instance difficulty; within-instance $\rho$ directly measures whether LP discriminates candidates for a fixed input, making it the relevant predictor of selection success.

_source line 18005 · 2026-05-20T13:38:48.443458+00:00_

1	\section{Theory \& Scaling Law}\label{sec:theory_scaling}
2	
3	\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\label{sec:mbr_theory}
4	
5	Entity-level construction (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\arg\max_{y \in \{y_1,\ldots,y_N\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\cup{=}\bigcup_i y_i$.
6	
7	\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\label{prop:construction_mbr}
8	Under entity-wise independence with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is
9	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mbr_construction}
10	\hat{y}_{\textup{MBR}} \!=\! \bigl\{e \!{\in}\! E_\cup \!:\! \hat{p}(e) \!>\! \tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha{+}1}\bigr\},\; \hat{p}(e) \!=\! \tfrac{1}{N} \textstyle\sum\nolimits_{i} \mathbf{1}[e \!{\in}\! y_i]
11	\end{equation}
12	The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields $\hat{p}(e) > \tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_proof}).
13	\end{proposition}
14	
15	\noindent\textbf{Remark.}
16	Set-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\cup| \leq 30$).
17	
18	Signal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\hat{p}_w(e) = \sum_{i} w_i \, \mathbf{1}[e {\in} y_i] / \sum_{i} w_i$.
19	Weighted MBR improves when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\rho{\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \S\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}).
20	
21	\subsection{Scaling Behavior}\label{sec:scaling}
22

…[truncated 4248 chars]

_source line 18011 · 2026-05-20T13:42:58.208277+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Test-time compute scaling---repeated sampling with selection or aggregation---yields large gains on discrete-answer reasoning and code generation, but named entity recognition (NER) produces \textit{sets} of entities with partial overlap, where standard aggregation mechanisms break down.
3	We evaluate five confidence signals for quality estimation (QE) and sample selection on four NER benchmarks across fine-tuned 4B\textendash 8B and zero-shot 72B models.
4	All signals achieve reliable QE ($\rho$ up to 0.45 on CoNLL-2003, area under the ROC curve [AUROC] up to 0.82), enabling selective prediction ($+5$--$8$\,pp F1 at 80\% coverage).
5	For instance-level selection, log-probability (LP) succeeds only where LP-quality alignment is strong, and gains are absent elsewhere despite large oracle headroom (the \textbf{correlation-selection gap}).
6	We identify a diagnostic cascade---instance degeneracy sets the ceiling on improvement, oracle headroom determines the opportunity, and LP compression limits exploitation---that persists under our 72B zero-shot configuration, providing the first systematic characterization of when test-time scaling succeeds or fails for NER and similar set-valued extraction tasks.
7	The diagnostic motivates entity-level \textit{construction}, adapting entity voting to stochastic sampling: LP-weighted aggregation improves F1 by $+1.40$\,pp on Few-NERD and $+1.02$\,pp on SciERC under fine-tuning (multi-seed mean, $p{<}0.005$). Construction and LP selection are complementary, governed by LP-quality alignment; a degeneracy decomposition confirms that SC gain vanishes when degeneracy exceeds 70\%.
8	\end{abstract}
9

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time compute scaling has emerged as a complement to model scaling \cite{snell2025scaling}: repeated sampling with majority voting \citep{wang2023selfconsistency}, best-of-$N$ with learned verifiers \cite{cobbe2021verifiers, brown2024monkeys}, and extended chain-of-thought \cite{muennighoff2025s1} yield large gains on reasoning and code generation, but the evidence concentrates on tasks with discrete answers or execution-based verification.
4	Named entity recognition (NER) poses a structurally different challenge: the output is a \textit{set} of entities, two samples may extract overlapping but non-identical sets, and there is no obvious majority to vote on.
5	We hypothesize this challenge generalizes to other set-valued extraction tasks (e.g., relation extraction), but verify only NER here.
6	
7	Prior work on confidence estimation for LLM-based IE has conflated \textit{quality estimation} (which inputs does the model handle reliably?) with \textit{sample selection} (which of $N$ outputs is best?).
8	Existing self-consistency methods \cite{wang2023selfconsistency, chen2024usc, taubenfeld2025cisc, kang2025selfcertainty} assume outputs can be grouped by equivalence, an assumption that breaks for entity sets with partial overlap.
9	Unlike \citet{yao2026consistency}, who demonstrate that self-consistency correlates with task accuracy, we provide the first systematic empirical characterization of \textit{when and why} test-time scaling via LP-based selection succeeds or fails for NER, diagnosing degeneracy and compression as the two blocking mechanisms.
10	
11	A key distinction structures our analysis: four of five signals (Soft Jaccard, voting confidence, Fleiss' $\kappa$, exact match) are instance-level aggregates that assign identical scores to all $N$ candidates for a given input; only log-probability (LP) is natively per-sample.
12	All signals achieve reliable quality estimation ($\rho$ up to 0.45, AUROC up t
…[truncated 3754 chars]

1	\section{Theory \& Scaling Law}\label{sec:theory_scaling}
2	
3	\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\label{sec:mbr_theory}
4	
5	Entity-level construction (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\arg\max_{y \in \{y_1,\ldots,y_N\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\cup{=}\bigcup_i y_i$.
6	
7	\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\label{prop:construction_mbr}
8	Under entity-wise independence with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is
9	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mbr_construction}
10	\hat{y}_{\textup{MBR}} \!=\! \bigl\{e \!{\in}\! E_\cup \!:\! \hat{p}(e) \!>\! \tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha{+}1}\bigr\},\; \hat{p}(e) \!=\! \tfrac{1}{N} \textstyle\sum\nolimits_{i} \mathbf{1}[e \!{\in}\! y_i]
11	\end{equation}
12	The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields $\hat{p}(e) > \tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_proof}).
13	\end{proposition}
14	
15	\noindent\textbf{Remark.}
16	Set-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\cup| \leq 30$).
17	
18	Signal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\hat{p}_w(e) = \sum_{i} w_i \, \mathbf{1}[e {\in} y_i] / \sum_{i} w_i$.
19	Weighted MBR improves when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\rho{\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \S\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}).
20	
21	\subsection{Scaling Behavior}\label{sec:scaling}
22	
23	We test whether the gap persists across scales and model families using Qwen2.5 (7B fine-tuned, 72B zero-shot and few-shot) a

1055-Only 5 RE instances with gold relations flip from LP failure (constrained) to LP success (free-form), with mean gold relation count 3.67 (well above the population mean of 1.86).
1056-These instances show large constrained LP errors ($\Delta{=}{-}0.430$) that reverse under free-form decoding ($\Delta{=}{+}0.503$), but their rarity (5/397) confirms that the RE selection flip is driven by aggregate variance reallocation rather than systematic instance-level correction.
1057-
1058:\section{$N$-Scaling Detailed Analysis}\label{app:n_scaling}
1059-
1060-Tables~\ref{tab:n-scaling}--\ref{tab:sigma-comparison} provide detailed $N$-scaling analysis on Qwen3-8B SciERC with 3-seed statistics.
1061-
--
1129-Doubling $N$ reduces cross-seed $\sigma$ by 32\% on NER and 24\% on RE (Table~\ref{tab:sigma-comparison}), with the largest reductions for SJ ($-43\%$ NER) and EM ($-59\%$ NER); additional samples stabilize structural signals more than LP.
1130-
1131-\paragraph{$N$-scaling: selection headroom vs.\ selection gain.}
1132:Table~\ref{tab:n_scaling_selection} traces the effect of sample count on degeneracy, oracle headroom, and per-signal selection gain.
1133-Oracle headroom grows roughly linearly with $N$ ($+4.4$~pp at $N{=}2$ to $+20.0$~pp at $N{=}32$).
1134-LP selection improves monotonically with $N$ ($+0.39$~pp at $N{=}8$ to $+0.95$~pp at $N{=}32$), but even at $N{=}32$, where degeneracy falls to 4\% and oracle headroom reaches ${\sim}20$~pp, the best signal captures less than 5\% of the available improvement.
1135-Degeneracy drops monotonically (43\% at $N{=}2$ to 4\% at $N{=}32$), yet reduced degeneracy does not unlock selection, consistent with the LoRA-rank ablation finding (\S\ref{sec:robustness_main}).

1	# Inverse Scaling Analysis for Structured Self-Consistency
2	
3	Date: 2026-05-20
4	
5	## Question
6	
7	Does increasing the number of samples N ever *decrease* entity-level F1 for any construction method? If so, which methods and under what conditions?
8	
9	## Setup
10	
11	- Configs: 6 (2 models x 3 datasets)
12	- Methods: majority_vote, lp_weighted, vc_weighted, sj_weighted, theta2n, uniform
13	- Threshold: majority_vote uses theta=0.5 (fixed); lp/vc/sj_weighted and theta2n use theta=2/N (adaptive); uniform uses theta=0.25 (fixed)
14	- Metric: entity-level micro F1 (pooled TP/FP/FN)
15	- Significance: paired bootstrap (B=5000-10000), 95% CI
16	
17	## Results
18	
19	**58 inverse scaling transitions detected, 40 statistically significant.**
20	
21	### Inverse Scaling Cases
22	
23	| Config | Method | N1->N2 | F1(N1) | F1(N2) | Drop | 95% CI | Sig? |
24	|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|------|--------|------|

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/experiments.tex:202:LP $\rho$ drops without fine-tuning (Table~\ref{tab:scale_comparison}) and LP selection fails on all datasets. Structural signals generalize (Few-NERD VC $\rho{=}0.416$). Degeneracy increases (Few-NERD: $46.2$\% vs.\ $13.0$\%; CoNLL: $67.7$\% vs.\ $61.7$\%); this comparison simultaneously varies model family, regime, and quantization, so individual effects cannot be disentangled.

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	NER exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$) but aggregation fails: a degeneracy decomposition ($\theta_{\text{crit}}{=}0.70$; \S\ref{sec:theory}) formalizes when SC becomes futile.
4	Entity-level construction partially closes the gap ($+1.40$\,pp Few-NERD, $+1.02$\,pp SciERC); a trained verifier achieves AUC${=}0.92$ but yields only $+0.30$\,pp via OOF construction, with negative cross-dataset transfer---content-aware methods \cite{cobbe2021verifiers} may bridge this.
5

1187	\paragraph{Oracle ceiling scaling law.}
1188	Oracle F1 follows $F_1(N) = a - b \cdot N^{-c}$, with $R^2 > 0.999$ across all model--dataset fits (Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_scaling}).
1189	The exponent $c$ varies with dataset complexity: FewNERD ($c{\approx}0.61$) scales comparably to reasoning-task estimates ($c{\approx}0.5$--$0.7$; \citealp{brown2024monkeys}), while SciERC converges more slowly ($c{\approx}0.35$), consistent with complex IE producing less marginal diversity per additional sample.
1190	CoNLL's narrow headroom ($b{=}0.04$--$0.10$) makes $c$ unreliable (inter-model variance $2.5{\times}$); we report only $a$ and $b$ for CoNLL.
1191	A single stochastic sample ($N{=}1$, $T{=}1.0$) underperforms greedy by 1--3\,pp because sampling noise outweighs diversity; $N{\geq}4$ is needed to match greedy.
1192

The ceiling grows monotonically with $N$, but the correlation-selection gap (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}) limits how much is actually recoverable.}

1054	\paragraph{Flip instances.}
1055	Only 5 RE instances with gold relations flip from LP failure (constrained) to LP success (free-form), with mean gold relation count 3.67 (well above the population mean of 1.86).
1056	These instances show large constrained LP errors ($\Delta{=}{-}0.430$) that reverse under free-form decoding ($\Delta{=}{+}0.503$), but their rarity (5/397) confirms that the RE selection flip is driven by aggregate variance reallocation rather than systematic instance-level correction.

115	Since instance-level selection fails, we present approaches that extract value from multi-sample generation without sample ranking.
116	
117	\paragraph{Entity-level construction.}
118	We aggregate entities across samples, retaining those exceeding $\theta{=}2/N$, with LP-weighted or uniform scoring ($\theta$ stable in $[0.15, 0.35]$; Appendix~\ref{app:theta_sweep}). In zero-shot, LP-weighting is necessary (SciERC $+1.5$\,pp; \S\ref{sec:scaling}).

  "content": "\\section{Phase Transitions}\\label{sec:theory_scaling}\n\n\\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\\label{sec:mbr_theory}\n\nEntity-level construction (\\S\\ref{sec:prescriptive}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \\cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\\arg\\max_{y \\in \\{y_1,\\ldots,y_N\\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\\cup{=}\\bigcup_i y_i$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\\label{prop:construction_mbr}\nUnder entity-wise independence with utility $u(\\hat{y}, y') = |\\hat{y} \\cap y'| - \\alpha|\\hat{y} \\setminus y'|$ ($\\alpha \\geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} \\!=\\! \\bigl\\{e \\!{\\in}\\! E_\\cup \\!:\\! \\hat{p}(e) \\!>\\! \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\; \\hat{p}(e) \\!=\\! \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i} \\mathbf{1}[e \\!{\\in}\\! y_i]\n\\end{equation}\nThe special case $\\alpha{=}1$ yields $\\hat{p}(e) > \\tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:mbr_proof}).\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).\n\nSignal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\\hat{p}_w(e) = \\sum_{i} w_i \\, \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i] / \\sum_{i} w_i$.\nWeighted MBR improves when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\\rho{\\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\\S\\ref{sec:prescriptive}).\n\n\\subsection{Threshold Sensitivity and Scaling}\\label{sec:scaling}\n\nOracle F1 follows $F_1(N) = a - b \\cdot N^{-c}$ ($R^2 > 0.999$; Figure~\\ref{fig:oracle_scaling}): Few-NERD ($c{\\approx}0.61$) scales comparably to reasoning tasks \\cite{brown2024monkeys}, while SciERC converges more slowly ($c{\\approx}0.35$).\nThe ceiling grows, but confidence-based aggregation recovers ${<}1$\\,pp---a persistent aggregation gap.\nThe gap persists across scales: LP $\\rho$ drops without fine-tuning, structural signals generalize (Few-NERD VC $\\rho{=}0.416$), and degeneracy is model-family-dependent rather than scale-dependent (Qwen2.5: $31.7$--$46.2$\\% vs.\\ Qwen3: $13.0$\\% on Few-NERD; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale}).\n\n\\paragraph{Inverse scaling under adaptive thresholds.}\nAcross methods and configurations, increasing $N$ \\textit{decreases} construction F1 in 40 of 58 observed transitions (paired bootstrap, $p{<}0.05$; Appendix~\\ref{app:n_scaling}).\nMethods using $\\theta{=}2/N$ are most affected: at $N{=}16$, $\\theta{=}0.125$ admits entities appearing in just 2 of 16 samples, with the worst case dropping $-3.45$\\,pp (CoNLL, $N{=}8{\\to}16$).\nMajority vote ($\\theta{=}\\tfrac{1}{2}$, the MBR-optimal threshold from Proposition~\\ref{prop:construction_mbr}) shows 0 significant inverse cases: a fixed proportional threshold prevents over-inclusion regardless of $N$.\nFew-shot demonstrations improve QE (CoNLL SJ $\\rho$ $.30{\\to}.44$) but not construction (all n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming signal quality alone is insufficient when threshold or alignment is misspecified.\n\n\\subsection{Degeneracy Decomposition}\\label{sec:theory}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Degeneracy Decomposition.}\\label{thm:degeneracy_bound}\nFor any aggregation method $A$: $\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A)] \\leq (1 - D) \\cdot G_{\\textup{nd}}$,\nwhere $D$ is the degeneracy rate and $G_{\\textup{nd}}$ the oracle gap on non-degenerate instances.\nFor signal-based methods with alignment $\\alpha \\in [0,1]$, this tightens to $(1{-}D) \\cdot G_{\\textup{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha$ (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:bound_proof}).\n\n\\noindent With $G_{\\textup{nd}}{\\approx}14.8$\\,pp and $\\alpha{\\approx}0.068$, the critical threshold is $\\theta_{\\textup{crit}}{=}0.70$: when $D{>}70$\\%, SC gain falls below $0.3$\\,pp.\nAcross 16 configurations, all gains respect the bound (ratio 3--12\\%; Figure~\\ref{fig:bound}); the bound is loose because $\\alpha$---not $D$---is the bottleneck.\nTightening requires improving within-instance alignment, which neither signal combination (\\S\\ref{sec:robustness_main}) nor verifier-based construction (\\S\\ref{sec:verifier}) achieves.\n"
}

    "content": "\\section{Phase Transitions}\\label{sec:theory_scaling}\n\n\\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\\label{sec:mbr_theory}\n\nEntity-level construction (\\S\\ref{sec:prescriptive}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \\cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\\arg\\max_{y \\in \\{y_1,\\ldots,y_N\\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\\cup{=}\\bigcup_i y_i$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\\label{prop:construction_mbr}\nUnder entity-wise independence with utility $u(\\hat{y}, y') = |\\hat{y} \\cap y'| - \\alpha|\\hat{y} \\setminus y'|$ ($\\alpha \\geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} \\!=\\! \\bigl\\{e \\!{\\in}\\! E_\\cup \\!:\\! \\hat{p}(e) \\!>\\! \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\; \\hat{p}(e) \\!=\\! \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i} \\mathbf{1}[e \\!{\\in}\\! y_i]\n\\end{equation}\nThe special case $\\alpha{=}1$ yields $\\hat{p}(e) > \\tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:mbr_proof}).\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).\n\nSignal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\\hat{p}_w(e) = \\sum_{i} w_i \\, \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i] / \\sum_{i} w_i$.\nWeighted MBR improves when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\\rho{\\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\\S\\ref{sec:prescriptive}).\n\n\\subsection{Threshold Sensitivity and Scaling}\\label{sec:scaling}\n\nOracle F1 follows $F_1(N) = a - b \\cdot N^{-c}$ ($R^2 > 0.999$; Figure~\\ref{fig:oracle_scaling}): Few-NERD ($c{\\approx}0.61$) scales comparably to reasoning tasks \\cite{brown2024monkeys}, while SciERC converges more slowly ($c{\\approx}0.35$).\nThe ceiling grows, but confidence-based aggregation recovers ${<}1$\\,pp---a persistent aggregation gap.\nThe gap persists across scales: LP $\\rho$ drops without fine-tuning, structural signals generalize (Few-NERD VC $\\rho{=}0.416$), and degeneracy is model-family-dependent rather than scale-dependent (Qwen2.5: $31.7$--$46.2$\\% vs.\\ Qwen3: $13.0$\\% on Few-NERD; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale}).\n\n\\paragraph{Inverse scaling under adaptive thresholds.}\nAcross methods and configurations, increasing $N$ \\textit{decreases} construction F1 in 40 of 58 observed transitions (paired bootstrap, $p{<}0.05$; Appendix~\\ref{app:n_scaling}).\nMethods using $\\theta{=}2/N$ are most affected: at $N{=}16$, $\\theta{=}0.125$ admits entities appearing in just 2 of 16 samples, with the worst case dropping $-3.45$\\,pp (CoNLL, $N{=}8{\\to}16$).\nMajority vote ($\\theta{=}\\tfrac{1}{2}$, the MBR-optimal threshold from Proposition~\\ref{prop:construction_mbr}) shows 0 significant inverse cases: a fixed proportional threshold prevents over-inclusion regardless of $N$.\nFew-shot demonstrations improve QE (CoNLL SJ $\\rho$ $.30{\\to}.44$) but not construction (all n.s.; Appendix~\\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming signal quality alone is insufficient when threshold or alignment is misspecified.\n\n\\subsection{Degeneracy Decomposition}\\label{sec:theory}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Degeneracy Decomposition.}\\label{thm:degeneracy_bound}\nFor any aggregation method $A$: $\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A)] \\leq (1 - D) \\cdot G_{\\textup{nd}}$,\nwhere $D$ is the degeneracy rate and $G_{\\textup{nd}}$ the oracle gap on non-degenerate instances.\nFor signal-based methods with alignment $\\alpha \\in [0,1]$, this tightens to $(1{-}D) \\cdot G_{\\textup{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha$ (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:bound_proof}).\n\n\\noindent With $G_{\\textup{nd}}{\\approx}14.8$\\,pp and $\\alpha{\\approx}0.068$, the critical threshold is $\\theta_{\\textup{crit}}{=}0.70$: when $D{>}70$\\%, SC gain falls below $0.3$\\,pp.\nAcross 16 configurations, all gains respect the bound (ratio 3--12\\%; Figure~\\ref{fig:bound}); the bound is loose because $\\alpha$---not $D$---is the bottleneck.\nTightening requires improving within-instance alignment, which neither signal combination (\\S\\ref{sec:robustness_main}) nor verifier-based construction (\\S\\ref{sec:verifier}) achieves.\n"
  }
}

Hdr.\\ = oracle $-$ greedy (pp). Degen.: instance degeneracy (\\%). $^\\star$Qwen2.5; unmarked: Qwen3. $^\\dagger$Not computed.}\n\\label{tab:scale_comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Regime & Greedy & Hdr. & Degen. & VC & SJ & FK & LP \\\\\n\\midrule\nSciERC & 8B FT  & .644 & 13.0 & 17.8 & .379 & .360 & .267 & .205 \\\\\n       & 72B ZS$^\\star$ & .399 & 15.0 & 21.0 & .277 & .217 & .227 & .081 \\\\\n\\midrule\nFew-NERD & 7B FT$^\\star$ & .784 & 9.2 & 31.7 & .404 & .388 & .315 & .346 \\\\\n         & 8B FT & .748 & 12.4 & 13.0 & .386 & .300 & .313 & .220 \\\\\n         & 72B ZS$^\\star$ & .628 & ---$^\\dagger$ & 46.2 & .416 & .345 & ---$^\\dagger$ & .119 \\\\\n\\midrule\nCoNLL  & 8B FT  & .908 & ${\\approx}$4 & 61.7 & .424 & .436 & .400 & .302 \\\\\n       & 72B ZS$^\\star$ & .712 & 5.3 & 67.7 & .297 & .304 & .302 & $-$.002 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nLP $\\rho$ drops without fine-tuning (Table~\\ref{tab:scale_comparison}) and LP selection fails on all 72B zero-shot datasets. Structural signals generalize better (Few-NERD VC $\\rho{=}0.416$). Degeneracy increases at 72B (Few-NERD: $46.2$\\% vs.\\ $13.0$\\%; CoNLL: $67.7$\\% vs.\\ $61.7$\\%); the gap persists under this specific 72B zero-shot configuration, though scale, training regime, and quantization effects are confounded (see Limitations).\nLP-weighted construction yields $+1.53$\\,pp on SciERC 72B ZS ($p{=}0.008$, CI [$+0.25$, $+2.82$]), recovering $10.2$\\% of headroom. Construction fails on Few-NERD ($-0.20$\\,pp) and CoNLL ($-0.37$\\,pp) where degeneracy exceeds $46$\\%---consistent with FT-regime degeneracy gating.\n\n\\paragraph{Few-shot demonstrations improve QE but not construction.}\nAdding 3-shot demonstrations to 72B improves greedy on SciERC and CoNLL ($+3.3$--$6.2$\\,pp) but not Few-NERD ($-0.9$\\,pp), and strengthens structural QE (CoNLL SJ $\\rho$ $.304{\\to}.442$, LP $-.002{\\to}.106$). Yet construction is ineffective across all three datasets (SciERC $-0.08$\\,pp, CoNLL $+0.03$\\,pp, Few-NERD $+0.02$\\,pp; all n.s.). On SciERC, this failure despite low degeneracy shows the bottleneck is regime-dependent (Appendix~\\ref{app:fs_error}); on Few-NERD, high FS degeneracy ($45.2$\\%) places it in the expected failure regime.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneracy is model-family-dependent, not scale-dependent.}\nQwen2.5 exhibits systematically higher degeneracy than Qwen3 on Few-NERD (7B--72B: $31.7$--$46.2$\\% vs.\\ Qwen3-8B: $13.0$\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:scale_comparison}), confirming degeneracy is shaped by architecture rather than parameter count. Construction fails for all Qwen2.5 configurations on Few-NERD (both n.s.); 3$\\to$5 epochs triples degeneracy within the same model (\\S\\ref{sec:fewnerd}).\n\n\\section{Temperature Ablation}\\label{app:t_ablation}"
}

1	\section{Phase Transitions}\label{sec:theory_scaling}
2	
3	\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\label{sec:mbr_theory}
4	
5	Entity-level construction (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\arg\max_{y \in \{y_1,\ldots,y_N\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\cup{=}\bigcup_i y_i$.
6	
7	\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\label{prop:construction_mbr}
8	Under entity-wise independence with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is
9	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mbr_construction}
10	\hat{y}_{\textup{MBR}} \!=\! \bigl\{e \!{\in}\! E_\cup \!:\! \hat{p}(e) \!>\! \tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha{+}1}\bigr\},\; \hat{p}(e) \!=\! \tfrac{1}{N} \textstyle\sum\nolimits_{i} \mathbf{1}[e \!{\in}\! y_i]
11	\end{equation}
12	The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields $\hat{p}(e) > \tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_proof}).
13	\end{proposition}
14	
15	\noindent\textbf{Remark.}
16	Set-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\cup| \leq 30$).
17	
18	Signal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\hat{p}_w(e) = \sum_{i} w_i \, \mathbf{1}[e {\in} y_i] / \sum_{i} w_i$.
19	Weighted MBR improves when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\rho{\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \S\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\S\ref{sec:prescriptive}).
20	
21	\subsection{Threshold Sensitivity and Scaling}\label{sec:scaling}
22	
23	Oracle F1 follows $F_1(N) = a - b \cdot N^{-c}$ ($R^2 > 0.999$; Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_scaling}): Few-NERD ($c{\

1	\section{Why Single-Signal Selection Fails}\label{sec:selection_gap}
2	
3	\paragraph{Definitional vs.\ empirical.}
4	That four of five signals assign identical scores to all candidates from the same input is definitional (\S\ref{sec:formulation}): instance-level aggregates estimate quality across inputs but cannot rank candidates within an instance.
5	The empirical findings are twofold: (a)~LP---the only per-sample signal---suffers compression under constrained decoding (median within-instance range 0.041 nats; 60\% of SciERC instances below this threshold; Appendix~\ref{app:logprob_ties}), and (b)~even where LP varies, selection gains depend on LP-quality alignment rather than degeneracy alone.
6	Cross-instance $\rho$ (Table~\ref{tab:quality_estimation}) is attenuated by varying instance difficulty; within-instance $\rho$ directly measures whether LP discriminates candidates for a fixed input, making it the relevant predictor of selection success.
7	
8	\begin{table}[t]
9	\centering
10	\small
